and set τ0 = 0. This is again an alternating polynomial in the remaining variables.

Lemma 31.1 (The conformal Euler homotopy). If P ∈ Ce n is homogeneous of polynomial degree w,
then
                                (dτ + τ d)P = (w − n)P.

Proof. Evaluate τ dP by writing the last variable in (31.1) as t. Terms merging two of the original
n variables occur in dτ P with the opposite sign: the former has the exterior factor (−1)n , the latter
(−1)n−1 . They cancel before any specialization of the other variables.
   The remaining pairs merge λi with t. Moving the merged first argument back to its ith position
gives (−1)i−1 . Together with the differential sign (−1)i+n+1 and the sign (−1)n in τn+1 , the result
is positive. Their total is therefore
                   n
                   X                                                               X
                         ∂t ((λi − t)P (λ1 , . . . , λi + t, . . . , λn ))|t=0 =       λi ∂i P − nP.
                   i=1                                                             i

Euler’s polynomial identity gives wP for the first sum. This proves the formula. For n = 0
both sides are zero on the constant space. As a sign check, for the one-cochain P (λ) = λ2 ,
dP = −(λ1 − λ2 )(λ1 + λ2 )2 and τ2 dP = λ21 .

   For w ̸= n, division by w − n contracts the entire homogeneous subcomplex. Characteristic
zero is essential here. A cocycle in that part is the boundary of (w − n)−1 τ P . Consequently only
polynomials of degree exactly n can contribute to basic cohomology.


31.3      All basic cohomology groups
An alternating polynomial is divisible by every λi − λj . These distinct irreducibles are relatively
prime as prime factors in the polynomial UFD, so it is divisible by their product
                                       Y
                               ∆n =          (λi − λj ),      deg ∆n = n(n − 1)/2.
                                       i<j

For a degree-n cochain to survive, one needs n(n − 1)/2 ≤ n. Thus only n = 0, 1, 2, 3 remain.
  In degree zero the constants survive. In degree one the degree-one polynomial space is ℸλ1 . In
degree two the degree-two alternating space is ℸ(λ21 − λ22 ), and

                                                  dλ1 = −(λ21 − λ22 ).

                                                           135
Chapter 31. The complete Virasoro conformal cohomology calculation


This is an isomorphism between those two one-dimensional spaces, so neither contributes cohomology.
In degree three the only degree-three alternating polynomial is a scalar multiple of

                                 ∆3 = (λ1 − λ2 )(λ1 − λ3 )(λ2 − λ3 ).

Its differential is alternating in four variables and has polynomial degree four. Such a polynomial
must vanish, since ∆4 already has degree six. It is not a boundary: a potential boundary comes
from a degree-two polynomial in the two-cochain space, but that entire space was the image of dλ1 ,
so its next differential is zero.

Theorem 31.2 (Basic Virasoro conformal cohomology). For the stated centreless Lie conformal
algebra and trivial coefficient module,
                                                   (
                                   en                  ℸ,   n = 0, 3,
                                   H (Vir, ℸ) =
                                                       0,   otherwise.

Representatives are 1 and ∆3 . The basic cup product is the unshuffle exterior product of polynomial
                                                                                              e 6.
cochains. Its degree-three generator has square zero; this also follows from the vanishing of H

   Every arity and every polynomial degree has been included: the contraction removes the
nonresonant degrees and divisibility removes the remaining high arities. This is not a finite-window
extrapolation.


31.4     Reduction by total translation
On Ce n let ∂ denote multiplication by λ1 + · · · + λn . This commutes with d: merging two variables
preserves their total sum. The reduced complex is

                                           C n = Ce n /∂ Ce n .
                                                                         P
It is the alternating polynomial operation space on the hyperplane i λi = 0. In degree zero,
∂ Ce 0 = 0; for n ≥ 1, multiplication by the nonzero polynomial i λi is injective. That exception is
                                                               P

important.
   There is a short exact sequence of complexes

                                  0 −→ ∂ Ce • −→ Ce • −→ C • −→ 0.

The first complex is isomorphic to the positive-degree part of Ce • , because its degree-zero term is
zero and the differential out of degree zero in the original complex was already zero. Hence it has
cohomology only in degree three, one-dimensional and represented by (λ1 + λ2 + λ3 )∆3 .
   Its inclusion in the basic complex induces zero on that group: the representative has polynomial
degree four in cochain degree three, so the Euler contraction makes it exact. In fact the primitive is
displayed by the direct identity

                                 d(λ31 − λ32 ) = −(λ1 + λ2 + λ3 )∆3 .                           (31.2)

The long exact sequence now gives the entire reduced answer.



                                                  136
Chapter 31. The complete Virasoro conformal cohomology calculation


Theorem 31.3 (Reduced Virasoro conformal cohomology). With the same algebra and coefficients,
                                                    (
                                                        ℸ,   n = 0, 2, 3,
                                   H n (Vir, ℸ) =
                                                        0,   otherwise.

Representatives are 1, the class of λ31 − λ32 on λ1 + λ2 = 0, and the class of ∆3 on λ1 + λ2 + λ3 = 0.
The connecting homomorphism sends the degree-two class to minus the generator of H 3 (∂ C).   e

Proof. The long exact sequence, the basic calculation and the zero inclusion just proved give
one-dimensional groups in degrees two and three and zero in every other positive degree. The
degree-zero quotient is still ℸ. Equation (31.2) proves the representative and connecting assertion
in degree two. The degree-three quotient map induces an isomorphism on cohomology, giving its
representative. These arguments also prove non-exactness; it is not inferred merely from a nonzero
displayed polynomial.

   The class in degree two is the cubic central-extension class, up to its normalization: setting
λ2 = −λ1 gives 2λ31 . The linear central term is a change of splitting and is removed by a coboundary,
as computed independently in the native deformation chapter.


31.5      The comparison which this theorem does not make
This calculation uses a centreless rank-one Lie conformal algebra, its trivial coefficient module,
conformal antilinearity, and reduction by total translation. The annihilation Lie algebra, the positive
Witt subalgebra, the vertex envelope at a selected central charge, and a native diagonal centre
are not the same input. For example the positive Witt homology has classes in arbitrarily high
homological degree, whereas the complex just computed has the displayed bounded amplitude.
   A theorem identifying a native chiral Hochschild complex with this reduced conformal complex
would need a map and a quasi-isomorphism proof. The amplitude {0, 2, 3} cannot serve as that
proof. The corrected use of the classical calculation is therefore constructive: it supplies a fully
computed native conformal complex and its transgression, which a proposed centre comparison can
now be tested against, without changing the definition of the centre.




                                                    137
Chapter 32

Native chiral operations and the
remaining comparison

32.1      The operation spaces have different types
For right DX -modules on a smooth characteristic-zero curve, let jI denote the complement of the
pair diagonals in X I and ∆I the small diagonal. The native chiral operation space is

                            PIch (Mi ; N ) = HomDX I (jI∗ jI∗ ⊠i Mi , ∆I∗ N ) .

The star operation has source ⊠i Mi without that localization. The natural restriction from chiral
to star operations comes from the map of those sources. Pointwise tensor operations are a third
construction. No identification of these spaces follows from their having the same number of inputs.
   A chiral algebra is a Lie algebra for the chiral pseudotensor operations, with its specified unit. A
multiplication for an internal associative centralizer must therefore be supplied separately. For the
ordinary Lie algebra [h, e] = e, the bracket gives [h, [h, e]] = e but [[h, h], e] = 0. Jacobi is not the
associative identity. The same failure of type would occur if a chiral bracket were inserted as the
multiplication of the ordinary tensor bar.


32.2      The fixed-module deformation problem
Fix the right DX -module and unit of a chiral algebra. The chiral endomorphism operad composes
by the specified nested diagonal maps and localization operations. Use its operadic suspension and
alternating invariants to form the positive-arity deformation complex. For a degree-zero underlying
module the binary bracket has degree one in this suspended complex. The commutator of insertion
is the Nijenhuis–Richardson bracket; the nested/disjoint insertion proof gives its graded Jacobi
identity.
   If µ is the original chiral operation, [µ, µ] = 0 is chiral Jacobi. Deformations ν which vanish when
the fixed unit is inserted satisfy
                                            [µ, ν] + 12 [ν, ν] = 0.
The original unit operation is not itself a normalized zero-residue operation. The differential
preserves normalized deformations because the two remaining unit-residue insertions cancel by
D-linearity; it is not correct to delete the original unit term first. Over a formal parameter the

                                                   138
Chapter 32. Native chiral operations and the remaining comparison


obstruction proof of Theorem 3.2 applies to this actual convolution object. It classifies and tests
lifts; it does not assert that every obstruction vanishes.
   Tamarkin’s chiral deformation construction and cohomological chiral-Gerstenhaber theorem
provide related native inputs [9]. Their product, star bracket, pro-objects and unit conventions must
be retained. A cohomological product whose restriction is null-homotopic is not a strict chain-level
product with zero restriction. In the present cohomological convention a bracket of degree −1 on C
becomes degree zero on C[1], not C[−1].


32.3     A complete central conformal deformation
Let L generate a free ℸ[T ]-module and let C be central with T C = 0. Consider

                                    [Lλ L] = (T + 2λ)L + f (λ)C.

Sesquilinearity defines the bracket on derivatives. Skew symmetry makes f odd. The noncentral
Jacobi identity is the polynomial identity for (T + 2λ)L. The central part is

                          (λ + 2µ)f (λ) − (µ + 2λ)f (µ) = (λ − µ)f (λ + µ).

Differentiate in µ and put µ = 0 to obtain 3f (λ) − λf ′ (λ) = 2λf ′ (0). Coefficient comparison forces

                                          f (λ) = aλ + bλ3 .

Substitution verifies both monomials, so the condition is sufficient. Replacing L by L + cC changes
f by −2cλ. With the quotient generator and central element fixed, the cubic coefficient is the
extension class. The argument applies coefficientwise to formal series a(t), b(t) and gives the all-
orders deformation in this ansatz. It does not classify every deformation of the full Virasoro vertex
algebra.
   For currents, [xλ y] = [x, y] + λκ(x, y)C satisfies skew symmetry exactly when κ is symmetric. Its
central Jacobi equation is

                          λκ(x, [y, z]) − µκ(y, [x, z]) = (λ + µ)κ([x, y], z).

This is exactly invariance of κ, with the second coefficient following by skew symmetry of the Lie
bracket. Thus the invariant pairing and level are genuine operation data, not values inferred from a
character.


32.4     What the new diagonal theorem supplies
Theorems 13.2 and 13.3 supply an actual higher centre comparison in an explicitly constructed
coefficient category. Theorem 18.1 computes a nonconstant ramified family there, with all internal
associative cochain operations retained. The acyclic reconstruction and support complexes of
Chapter 10 supply all-arity collision coefficients and their nontrivial derived fibres. The boundary
kernel of Chapter 9 supplies the degree-sensitive supported target. These are substantive interfaces
with explicit maps.
   They do not identify an arbitrary Beilinson–Drinfeld chiral algebra with an associative algebra
in this coefficient category. Theorem 11.3 now identifies the analytic interior coefficient sheaf with

                                                  139
Chapter 32. Native chiral operations and the remaining comparison


the actual partial de Rham–Dolbeault complex on Conf I (C × R), in every finite arity. It does not
yet supply the boundary-coloured state operations or identify an algebraic finite-pole subcomplex
with all analytic holomorphic functions. The complete internal associative action is therefore not
relabelled as the native mixed theorem.


32.5     The coefficient and state comparisons are now separate maps
The interior coefficient map is no longer just a proposed identification. With FI = Conf I (C × R),
the canonical projection pI remembers the real order within equal-holomorphic-coordinate blocks.
Pullback gives
                                        OXIan ≃ RpI∗ AHT .
The overlapwise convex homotopies and the Stein Dolbeault comparison prove that statement. After
topologization the coefficient calculation becomes the complete configuration ring of R3 , including
its degree-two generators and three-label relation. This validates a geometric source of interior
operations, not a choice of their values on a state space.
   There is also an all-arity state-space coherence construction for the specified matrix-factorization
probes of Chapter 23. It starts from strict anchor-composition maps, transports them by an explicit
finite convolution gauge transformation, and reproduces the original pairwise maps with their
nonzero triangle homotopies. Its success uses an actual strict system and actual primitives. It is not
the assertion that the obstruction groups of every chiral algebra vanish.
   Finally the absolute centre restores holomorphic derivations. Its bracket [ηi , zj ] = δij prevents
treating those coordinate functions as scalar coefficients of its E2 structure. The native geometric
comparison must respect this distinction as well as the old coefficient-level idempotents. Passing
between relative and absolute centres is a map with a calculated cone, not a change of terminology.


32.6     The exact mixed theorem still requiring a construction
A native version needs a two-coloured geometric operation system SX whose boundary restriction
recovers the given chiral pseudotensor operations and whose interior coefficient system retains
the derived holomorphic–topological collision coefficients. It needs an actual object Zch (A) and a
comparison with the intended native chiral deformation or Hochschild object. Finally it needs a
morphism from a cofibrant resolution of SX to

                                           End(Zch (A), A),

including every unit, boundary transgression, composition homotopy and descent map, together
with the centralizing universal property in that same category.
   Defining SX to be the endomorphism operad of a preselected pair would make its action
tautological and would not compare it to the geometric source. Defining a centre by an ordinary
Hochschild formula without specifying the associative input would change the object. Naming
a Maurer–Cartan element without constructing its components would leave the action unproved.
None of those substitutions is used in this edition.
   The most immediate tests on a candidate comparison are now independent and explicit. It must
preserve the two branch idempotents in the coefficient fibre, remove cyclic tournament branches
by the support correction, retain the degree shift of the supported boundary module, obey the

                                                 140
Chapter 32. Native chiral operations and the remaining comparison


arity-dependent input budget for complete operations, and carry the root relation’s homotopy
bδ = mtβ. The strict Gerstenhaber shortcut in Chapter 16 fails even before these geometric
requirements are combined.


32.7     A unital example of what the boundary does not determine
Even in the ordinary internal setting, the universal centre is not every bulk algebra which can
act. Take the boundary A = ℸ. Both B0 = ℸ and B1 = ℸ[u], with their commutative higher
structures, have central actions on A: the latter is f (u) · a = f (0)a. Its map to Z(A) = ℸ is a unital
augmentation and is not a quasi-isomorphism. The two boundary algebras agree while the two
acting algebras have different cohomology. The augmentation ideal acts trivially; the unit does
not. This repairs the stronger, false wording that would demand a null action of an entire unital
decoupled sector. A physical open–closed equivalence has to establish equivalence of its actual
centralizing map, not merely its existence.


32.8     A finite quantum obstruction
There is a parallel danger in passing from a classical construction to a quantum one. Let

              O = ℸ[ξ1 , ξ2 ] ⊗ Λ(x1 , x2 ),   |ξi | = −2,   |xi | = 1,   ∆ = ∂x1 ∂ξ1 + ∂x2 ∂ξ2 .

Use left odd derivatives and the BV bracket generated by this degree-one square-zero operator. For
S = ξ2 x1 x2 , one has S 2 = 0, ∆S = −x1 , and (∆S)S = 0. Hence the classical master equation holds.
    Every degree-zero candidate first correction is S1 = c + (aξ1 + bξ2 )x1 x2 . Direct multiplication
gives SS1 = cS; the terms in the generated bracket cancel, so {S, S1 } = 0 for every candidate. The
first quantum equation would require 0 + ∆S = −x1 = 0, impossible in this algebra. The obstruction
is an actual nonzero cohomology class for the supplied field content. Changing that content or the
coefficient algebra is new data, not an automatic consequence of the classical equation.


32.9     The logical endpoint of this edition
The reconstructed mathematical core consists of the proved word adjunction and reconstruction; the
module, quadratic and Morita comparisons, including the signed positive-weight module–comodule
equivalence and tensor–cotensor law; the complete operation models; the actual mixed configuration
coefficient comparison; relative and absolute order-chart diagonal centres with their distinct higher
scalar structures; the finite-etale refinement criterion; the dimension-dependent formal quantum
normal centre; and the all-arity coherent matrix-factorization probe family. The finite Hall, spectral
exchange, Cardy sewing and integral vacuum calculations retain their explicit operations and their
separate geometric domains. It is a single coherent development, rather than a claim that all its
objects are equivalent.
    The full native mixed comparison and the original all-genus, automorphic, Hall-specialization
and gravity identifications are not proved here. Their source hypotheses and scalar observations are
not inserted as conclusions. Appendix A records these dependencies so that later volumes cannot
silently cite this edition as a proof of them. This is a statement about the specific missing maps,
not a claim that their construction is impossible or that the programme should abandon them.

A.2      Corrections with exact locations
The supplied 121-page draft has an empty contents page and unresolved cross-references throughout;
the present source is rebuilt and cross-referenced independently rather than silently concatenated
with those pages. Its substantive limits in the preface and Chapter 19 are retained. In particular
its final pages state that the native mixed action has not been constructed; that limitation is not
turned into a theorem in this edition.
   In the supplied 281-page synthesis, Section 10.1, pp. 40–53, and Section 12.1, pp. 246–249,
several generalizations require correction. The factorizing subcategory is not a stable ambient for
pointwise sums. The singleton-supported Cauchy square has two nonempty summands at arity two.
A degreewise dual of the dual-number bar is not wrong in each positive weight; the issue is the
topology used to assemble weights. The overview’s common Gram determinant is replaced by the
distinct weight-four and weight-five determinants. The nonisomorphism of quantum and Jordan
algebras does not imply inequivalence of their graded module categories. The displayed affine-current
residue calculation with an omitted eleven-term operator is not certified by any ordinary tensor-bar
check.
   The synthesis’s normally ordered bar differential is not repaired by assigning an associator to
a minimal m3 . Chapter 3 gives the actual transfer and its hypotheses. A scalar augmentation
of a Weyl algebra or matrix algebra is impossible; Chapter 4 replaces it by actual modules or a
nonaugmented resolution. A scalar trace is not used as an augmentation. A level-reflection identity
is not used as a Koszul-dual identification.
   The later arithmetic reconstruction distinguishes native chiral operations, star operations and
pointwise tensor operations, and retains the degree shift in its boundary discussion. Those corrections
remain in Chapter 32. Its all-tree scale construction is retained only with a common simultaneous
Laurent bound; an arbitrary intermediate completion or a substitution of one for a formal scale is
not permitted.




                                                 143
Appendix A. Source-to-result register


A.3      New corrections to the tempting centre argument
The compact-generator theorem for order-chart coefficients must use the coherent commutative
algebra C, not only an associative Cech multiplication with an undeclared symmetric structure. Its
proof must establish generation for unbounded complexes; the residue-field argument does so. The
relative diagonal is open, not generally closed. Its multiplication C ⊗R C → C is a homological
epimorphism, while R → C is not asserted to be one. These distinctions are used in every step of
the new centre comparison.
   The resulting internal centre is not automatically the naive sheaf of cochains. The odd square-zero
example shows that scalar finiteness alone does not commute the centre with localization. The
bounded-coefficient theorem requires an ordinary finite-projective algebra and bounded coefficient
complex; it does not apply merely because a graded algebra is proper. The root family’s special base
change is proved with the Koszul coefficient model, not imported from a false general statement.
   The root family has even cohomology but its cochains do not have a strict Gerstenhaber product
compatible with all the naive generator relations. The explicit defect bδ = mtβ replaces that false
shortcut. Its special-fibre bracket proves the absence of an E∞ refinement of the relative centre.
This obstruction is not a claim that no higher model on cohomology can exist; any valid model
must retain higher operations.


A.4      Continuity with the preceding progress
The following distinctions and constructions are preserved, not retracted: the algebraic bar–cobar
counit without a quadratic hypothesis; the positive-weight unit and completion conventions; the
failure of cobar to preserve all underlying coalgebra quasi-isomorphisms; the split alignment projector
without a global interchange isomorphism; the three separate bar constructions; the difference
between augmentation dual, probe endomorphisms and diagonal centre; the two-chart idempo-
tent test; the cyclic tournament correction; complete brace input budgets; the perfect-diagonal
base-change criterion; parameter-scaled Chevalley differentials and the diagonal-centre Bockstein;
integral Massey torsion; divided Hasse operators and their digits; polarized matrix-factorization
endomorphisms; and the polynomial stress-tensor correlation formula.
   Some additional computations in prior sources, such as particular high-rank Lie-homology
classifications, the complete representation theory of Yangians beyond the proved RTT/PBW
and evaluation statements, or automorphic normalizations, are not re-proved in this edition. The
all-object, all-morphism monomial matrix-factorization calculation and the explicit root-probe
coherences, in contrast, are reconstructed in Chapter 23. They are not silently declared false or
deleted from the research programme, but they cannot be cited as checked here. Their original
source files, when present in the runtime, are recorded by the source manifest. The manifest is
evidence of file continuity, not a proof of their mathematical claims.


A.5      Dependencies of the principal new theorems
The relative order-chart centre uses finite affine descent, residue-field detection, compact-generator
Morita, the affine open diagonal, and the internal associative centre structure. It does not use the
native mixed conjecture. The root-family nonformality theorem uses the explicit periodic resolution,
carry cochain, proved special base change and the nonzero special-fibre bracket. It does not use the

                                                 144
Appendix A. Source-to-result register


order-chart result. The coupled centre theorem uses these two independent constructions and the
bounded-coefficient comparison. The polarized boundary calculation uses only its matrix differential
and regular-sequence Koszul complex. These separate dependencies make the tests noncircular.


A.6      The numbered source register and its limits
The accompanying Original_Claim_Register.json, CSV and searchable HTML locate 429 num-
bered headings in the exact 510-page source, including ordinary theorems, recognition and external
theorems, criteria, definitions, and 36 no-go headings. The latter include the original sequence of
24 and later chapter- and appendix-numbered restatements. Detection uses the displayed heading
fonts or explicit titled headings; ordinary sentences beginning with a theorem cross-reference are
excluded. Each entry retains its source PDF page, an excerpt, a reconstruction label, and a status.
   This register is not an assertion that 429 claims were individually proved. A branch-mapped entry
is a reading route, not a proof certification. An entry without an individually checked replacement
remains marked accordingly. Unnumbered assertions and equations are not exhaustively indexed by
this heading pass. The exact original PDF and its cryptographic hash remain in the package, so the
registry does not delete source material when it marks a comparison unresolved.
   The original mixed-operad proof in Chapter 5, PDF pp. 44–46, assumes the operad, its ad-
missibility, and the Quillen-equivalence comparison. A determinant-line sign makes two equal
coefficient composites cancel; it does not prove those coefficient composites equal. Chapter 29
proves the properly conditioned cancellation, Chapter 12 constructs actual scale-marked coefficient
substitutions, and Chapter 23 supplies an explicit all-arity state coherence in a specified family.
These replacements must not be combined by declaring the missing universal action solved.
   The original Chapter 12, PDF pp. 65–67, places a free tensor-cobar formula beside a higher-
categorical bar-left-adjoint statement and a blanket nilcompletion conclusion. Chapter 2 separates
those adjunctions and proves the algebraic counit without a quadratic or completion hypothesis.
Chapter 7 proves the positive-weight module comparison and its actual morphism-weight convention.
A higher-categorical completion theorem is not contradicted by that algebraic result; it has a
different construction and domain.
   The amplitude {0, 2, 3} is now proved in full for the reduced Virasoro conformal complex in
Chapter 31, by an all-arity Euler contraction and a short exact sequence. It remains unavailable as
the amplitude of an unspecified chiral centre. This is a corrected placement of an actual calculation,
not a subtraction from the programme’s mathematical content.


A.7      Build and verification repairs made in this edition
The recovered source included a Weyl-vacuum proof chapter which its master file never loaded.
That chapter is restored, and its references are now resolved. The previous compiler failure on
an undefined rational-number macro is repaired. The two Virasoro Gram matrices in the source
were correct; the failed symbolic test compared expanded polynomials with factored expressions by
structural equality. The test now expands the matrix difference and checks that each entry is zero.
It was not repaired by changing a correct target matrix to match an incorrect calculation.
   A further sign check during reconstruction concerns formal coordinate groups. Composition of
series as point maps has Lie bracket opposite to the usual derivation bracket. Chapter 26 fixes the


                                                145
Appendix A. Source-to-result register


corresponding negative infinitesimal convention for inverse substitution, and checks the descended
connection against that explicit representation. Neither this convention nor the module-bar shift
convention is left implicit.
   The Cardy calculation distinguishes the open contraction τB (hgB ) from a closed-sector genus
amplitude. For matrix blocks the latter is computed with the closed trace λ2 , not the open trace
λ Tr. The full matrix-unit calculation in Chapter 29 preserves both quantities and identifies their
different domains. Thus a valid trace formula is not discarded, but it is no longer presented as an
invariant of the wrong sewing theory.




                                               146
Appendix B

Reproducible finite verification

The four accompanying Python programs use exact integer, rational and symbolic arithmetic. It
checks finite instances of formulas proved in the text. It is not a formal proof assistant certificate
and does not verify every theorem of the book. Its report records actual parameter ranges and
assertion counts; no unperformed calculation is marked passed.
   The root tests check multiplication, the periodic factorization, the carry cocycle, the Euler defect,
and the displayed cochain brackets for multiple root orders. The two-root tests check the complete
differential’s square and the cup Leibniz identity on bounded cochain degrees. The order tests
enumerate tournaments and compare acyclic counts with factorials, and verify equality-block fibre
counts. The support tests check finite Koszul windows together with the maps that kill the spurious
lower-degree classes in the next window. The latter transition is essential: a finite window alone has
different homology from the infinite principal-part module.
   The boundary tests verify the polarized Clifford relations in several ranks, including characteristic
two. The bar tests check graded associativity cancellation and cut contractions on bounded words.
The conformal tests recompute the two Gram matrices and check Ward recursion against the cycle
formula at distinct rational points, including a degenerate Gram parameter. The exchange test
verifies the polynomial form of the rational Yang–Baxter identity and its scalar unitarity defect.


B.1     Further geometric, module, and field checks
The second suite checks all matrix-factorization composition types through potential degree nine, the
displayed three-probe homotopy and the finite gauge construction, symbolic Moyal associativity at
bounded order, socle derivations, the Cardy matrix identity, finite Hall counts, Gaudin commutators,
and the two-evaluation RTT identity. Its tournament enumeration runs through six labels. The
proof that order-chart coefficients model the mixed configuration space is the overlapwise contraction
of Chapter 11; tournament enumeration is not that proof.
   The third suite checks the three-level cluster substitutions and their barycentric maps, finite
orders of simultaneous inverse expansion, the variance of formal coordinate changes, Schwarzian
identities, and the signed conformal Euler homotopy. The basic and reduced Virasoro cohomology
theorem uses the all-degree contraction and alternating-polynomial divisibility, not extrapolation
from the tested arities.
   The fourth suite checks the module-bar sign comparison in every parity pattern through length six,


                                                  147
Appendix B. Reproducible finite verification


the twisting differential and comodule unit on bounded free words, and the polynomial conjugation
which recovers both constant fields of the full Wick–Ore model. These tests do not remove the
positive auxiliary-weight hypotheses of Chapter 7. Nor do they identify its homogeneous graded
centre with an unrestricted weight-mixing Hom.


B.2      Executed report
The executed reports contain 33839 successful assertions across 26 finite test groups. Assertions are
individual checks of specified identities or matrix entries, not counts of proved theorems.

                      Suite                                Groups    Assertions
                      Recovered and repaired core              12        13702
                      Geometry, probes, and exchange            8        12434
                      Cluster and conformal coherences          3            79
                      Module and field signs                    3         7624

The four machine-readable reports record the exact ranges. Their script and report SHA-256 hashes
are listed in VERIFICATION_SUMMARY.json.


B.3      Reading the bounds correctly
Successful checks at finitely many arities do not establish a complete operad action. In the text, the
brace and cut identities are proved by their all-arity insertion or tensor formulas. The checks test
those formulas and their signs. Similarly the cycle formula is proved through Wick contractions and
polynomial interpolation in the central charge; numerical marked-point checks are regressions, not
that proof.
   A finite truncation of an infinite cochain product can commute with localization even when the
full object does not. That counterexample is retained explicitly. A finite principal-part window has
lower Koszul classes which vanish on passage to a larger window; recording only its ranks would
misstate the colimit. A finite family fibre can have the correct dimensions and still have the wrong
multiplication, as both the order and boundary examples show. The verification suite therefore
includes maps and algebra products, not only dimension checks.
